AddEquiv.lpBCFdefMathlib.Analysis.Normed.Lp.LpEquiv, function or constant
Wording
no wording
English
$\operatorname{lpBCF}$ is an additive equivalence of $\SubtypeSet {x_{3}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoT {\mathrm{lp} (\lambda x_{4} . E , \operatorname{top})}{x_{3}})}$ and the set of bounded continuous functions of $x$ and $E$.
Formula
$\operatorname{lpBCF} \in \mathrm{AddEquiv} (\SubtypeSet {x_{3}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoAA {\mathrm{lp} (\lambda x_{4} . E , \operatorname{top})}{x_{3}})}, \mathrm{BoundedContinuousFunction} (x , E))$
French

Mathlib.Analysis.Normed.Lp.Matrix

Matrix.toLpLindefMathlib.Analysis.Normed.Lp.Matrix, function or constant
Wording
no wording
English
Let $p$ and $q$ be elements of $\mathrm{ENNReal}$. Then $\operatorname{toLpLin}$ applied to $p$ and $q$ is a linear equivalence of the set of matrixes of $n$ and $R$ and the set of linear maps of $\mathrm{WithLp}$ applied to $p$ and the set of functions from elements of $n$ to elements of $R$ and $\mathrm{WithLp}$ applied to $q$ and the set of functions from elements of $m$ to elements of $R$ defined as the composition of $\operatorname{toLin'}$ and the arrow congruence of the inverse of the linear equivalence of $p$ and the inverse of the linear equivalence of $q$.
Formula
$p , q \in \mathrm{ENNReal} \vdash \operatorname{toLpLin} (p , q):= \operatorname{trans} (\operatorname{toLin'} , \operatorname{arrowCongr} (\operatorname{symm} (\operatorname{linearEquiv} (p , R , n \rightarrow R)), \operatorname{symm} (\operatorname{linearEquiv} (q , R , m \rightarrow R))))\in \mathrm{LinearEquiv} (\operatorname{id} (R), \mathrm{Matrix} (m , n , R), \mathrm{LinearMap} (\operatorname{id} (R), \mathrm{WithLp} (p , n \rightarrow R), \mathrm{WithLp} (q , m \rightarrow R)))$
French
Soient $p$ et $q$ des éléments de $\mathrm{ENNReal}$. Alors $\operatorname{toLpLin}$ appliqué à $p$ et $q$ est une équivalence linéaire de l'ensemble de matrices de $n$ et $R$ et l'ensemble d'applications linéaires de $\mathrm{WithLp}$ appliqué à $p$ et l'ensemble de fonctions des éléments de $n$ à des éléments de $R$ et $\mathrm{WithLp}$ appliqué à $q$ et l'ensemble de fonctions des éléments de $m$ à des éléments de $R$ défini comme la composition de $\operatorname{toLin'}$ et la congruence de flèches de l'inverse de l'équivalence linéaire de $p$ et l'inverse de l'équivalence linéaire de $q$.

English

Then $\operatorname{toLpLinAlgEquiv}$ applied to $p$ is an algebra isomorphism of the set of matrixes of $n$ and $R$ and the set of ends of $R$ and $\mathrm{WithLp}$ applied to $p$ and the set of functions from elements of $n$ to elements of $R$ defined as the algebra isomorphism of $\operatorname{toLpLin}$ applied to $p$ and $p$.
Formula
$p \in \mathrm{ENNReal} \vdash \operatorname{toLpLinAlgEquiv} (p):= \operatorname{ofLinearEquiv} (\operatorname{toLpLin} (p , p), \mathrm{Matrix\_\_toLpLin\_one} (p), \mathrm{Matrix\_\_toLpLin\_mul} (p , p , p))\in \mathrm{AlgEquiv} (R , \mathrm{Matrix} (n , n , R), \operatorname{End} (R , \mathrm{WithLp} (p , n \rightarrow R)))$

MeasurableEquiv.toLpdefMathlib.Analysis.Normed.Lp.MeasurableSpace, function or constant
Wording
no wording
English
Let $p$ be an element of $\mathrm{ENNReal}$. Let $X$ be a set. Then $\operatorname{toLp}$ applied to $p$ and $X$ is a measurable equivalence of $X$ and $\mathrm{WithLp}$ applied to $p$ and $X$ defined as $\operatorname{mk}$ applied to the inverse of the equivalence of $p$ and $X$, $\mathrm{WithLp\_\_measurable\_toLp}$ applied to $p$ and $X$ and $\mathrm{WithLp\_\_measurable\_ofLp}$ applied to $p$ and $X$.
Formula
$p \in \mathrm{ENNReal} , X \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{toLp} (p , X):= \operatorname{mk} (\operatorname{symm} (\operatorname{equiv} (p , X)), \mathrm{WithLp\_\_measurable\_toLp} (p , X), \mathrm{WithLp\_\_measurable\_ofLp} (p , X))\in \mathrm{MeasurableEquiv} (X , \mathrm{WithLp} (p , X))$
French
Soit $p$ un élément de $\mathrm{ENNReal}$. Soit $X$ un ensemble. Alors $\operatorname{toLp}$ appliqué à $p$ et $X$ est une équivalence mesurable de $X$ et $\mathrm{WithLp}$ appliqué à $p$ et $X$ défini comme $\operatorname{mk}$ appliqué à l'inverse de l'équivalence de $p$ et $X$, $\mathrm{WithLp\_\_measurable\_toLp}$ appliqué à $p$ et $X$ et $\mathrm{WithLp\_\_measurable\_ofLp}$ appliqué à $p$ et $X$.

LinearIsometryEquiv.piLpCongrRightdefMathlib.Analysis.Normed.Lp.PiLp, function or constant
Wording
no wording
English
Let $p$ be an element of $\mathrm{ENNReal}$. Let $e$ be a function from elements $i$ of $x_{1}$ to linear isometry equivalences of $x_{2}$ applied to $i$ and $x_{3}$ applied to $i$. Then $\operatorname{piLpCongrRight}$ applied to $p$, $i$ and $e$ is a linear isometry equivalence of $\mathrm{PiLp}$ applied to $p$ and $x_{2}$ and $\mathrm{PiLp}$ applied to $p$ and $x_{3}$.
Formula
$p \in \mathrm{ENNReal} , e \in x_{1} \rightarrow \mathrm{LinearIsometryEquiv} (\operatorname{id} (x), x_{2} (i), x_{3} (i))\vdash \operatorname{piLpCongrRight} (p , i , e)\in \mathrm{LinearIsometryEquiv} (\operatorname{id} (x), \mathrm{PiLp} (p , x_{2}), \mathrm{PiLp} (p , x_{3}))$
French
Soit $p$ un élément de $\mathrm{ENNReal}$. Soit $e$ une fonction des éléments $i$ de $x_{1}$ à des équivalences d'isométries linéaires de $x_{2}$ appliqué à $i$ et $x_{3}$ appliqué à $i$. Alors $\operatorname{piLpCongrRight}$ appliqué à $p$, $i$ et $e$ est une équivalence d'isométries linéaire de $\mathrm{PiLp}$ appliqué à $p$ et $x_{2}$ et $\mathrm{PiLp}$ appliqué à $p$ et $x_{3}$.

Wording
no wording
English
Let $x$ be a set. Let $p$ be an element of $\mathrm{ENNReal}$. Let $x_{6}$ be a function from elements $i_{2}$ of $x_{2}$ and elements of $x_{3}$ applied to $i_{2}$ to sets. Then $\operatorname{piLpCurry}$ applied to $x$, $p$, $i_{2}$ and $x_{6}$ is a linear isometry equivalence of $\mathrm{PiLp}$ applied to $p$ and the function that maps $i$ to $x_{6}$ applied to $\operatorname{fst}$ applied to $i$ and $\operatorname{snd}$ applied to $i$ and $\mathrm{PiLp}$ applied to $p$ and the function that maps $i_{1}$ to $\mathrm{PiLp}$ applied to $p$ and $x_{6}$ applied to $i_{1}$.

French

PiLpdefMathlib.Analysis.Normed.Lp.PiLp, type
Wording
no wording
English
Let $p$ be an element of $\mathrm{ENNReal}$. Let $x_{1}$ be a function from elements of $x$ to sets. Then an element of $\mathrm{PiLp}$ applied to $p$ and $x_{1}$ is an element of the function that maps $p$ and $x_{1}$ to $\mathrm{WithLp}$ applied to $p$ and the set of functions from elements $i$ of $x$ to elements of $x_{1}$ applied to $i$.
Formula
$p \in \mathrm{ENNReal} , x_{1} \in x \rightarrow \mathrm{Set} \vdash \mathrm{PiLp} (p , x_{1}):= \lambda p , x_{1} . \mathrm{WithLp} (p , x \rightarrow x_{1} (i))$
French
Soit $p$ un élément de $\mathrm{ENNReal}$. Soit $x_{1}$ une fonction des éléments de $x$ à des ensembles. Alors un élément de $\mathrm{PiLp}$ appliqué à $p$ et $x_{1}$ est un élément de la fonction qui envoie $p$ et $x_{1}$ à $\mathrm{WithLp}$ appliqué à $p$ et l'ensemble de fonctions des éléments $i$ de $x$ à des éléments de $x_{1}$ appliqué à $i$.
PiLp.basisFundefMathlib.Analysis.Normed.Lp.PiLp, function or constant
Wording
the basis function of p (from the name)
English
Let $p$ be an element of $\mathrm{ENNReal}$. Let $x$ and $x_{1}$ be sets. Then the basis function of $p$ is a basis of $x$ and $\mathrm{PiLp}$ applied to $p$ and the function that maps $x_{3}$ to $x$ defined as the basis of the linear equivalence of $p$.

$p \in \mathrm{ENNReal} , x , x_{1} \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{basisFun} (p , x , x_{1}):= \operatorname{ofEquivFun} (\operatorname{linearEquiv} (p , x , x_{1} \rightarrow x))\in \operatorname{Basis} (x_{1} , x , \mathrm{PiLp} (p , \lambda x_{3} . x))$
French

lp.dualPairingdefMathlib.Analysis.Normed.Lp.lpHolder, function or constant
Wording

English

IsBoundedLinearMapstructureMathlib.Analysis.Normed.Operator.BoundedLinearMaps, predicate
Wording
f is a bounded linear map (from the name)
English
Let $x$ be a set. Let $f$ be a function from elements of $E$ to elements of $F$. Then we can say that $f$ is a bounded linear map.
Formula
$x \in \mathrm{Set} , f \in E \rightarrow F \vdash \mathrm{IsBoundedLinearMap} (x , f)\ \mathsf{prop}$
French
Soit $x$ un ensemble. Soit $f$ une fonction des éléments de $E$ à des éléments de $F$. Alors nous pouvons dire que $f$ est une application linéaire bornée.
IsBoundedLinearMap.toContinuousLinearMapdefMathlib.Analysis.Normed.Operator.BoundedLinearMaps, function or constant
Wording
the continuous linear map of f and hf (from the name)
English
Let $f$ be a function from elements of $E$ to elements of $F$ such that $f$ is a bounded linear map. Then the continuous linear map of $f$ and $\mathrm{hf}$ is a continuous linear map of $E$ and $F$.
Formula
$f \in E \rightarrow F , \mathrm{IsBoundedLinearMap} (x , f)\vdash \operatorname{toContinuousLinearMap} (f , \mathrm{hf})\in \mathrm{ContinuousLinearMap} (\operatorname{id} (x), E , F)$
French
Soit $f$ une fonction des éléments de $E$ à des éléments de $F$ telle que $f$ soit une application linéaire bornée. Alors l'application linéaire continue de $f$ et $\mathrm{hf}$ est une application linéaire continue de $E$ et $F$.
IsBoundedLinearMap.toLinearMapdefMathlib.Analysis.Normed.Operator.BoundedLinearMaps, function or constant
Wording
the linear map of f and h (from the name)
English
Let $f$ be a function from elements of $E$ to elements of $F$ such that $f$ is a bounded linear map. Then the linear map of $f$ and $h$ is a linear map of $E$ and $F$ defined as $\operatorname{mk'}$ applied to $f$ and $\operatorname{toIsLinearMap}$ applied to $h$.
Formula
$f \in E \rightarrow F , \mathrm{IsBoundedLinearMap} (x , f)\vdash \operatorname{toLinearMap} (f , h):= \operatorname{mk'} (f , \operatorname{toIsLinearMap} (h))\in \mathrm{LinearMap} (\operatorname{id} (x), E , F)$
French
Soit $f$ une fonction des éléments de $E$ à des éléments de $F$ telle que $f$ soit une application linéaire bornée. Alors l'application linéaire de $f$ et $h$ est une application linéaire de $E$ et $F$ défini comme $\operatorname{mk'}$ appliqué à $f$ et $\operatorname{toIsLinearMap}$ appliqué à $h$.

Formula

IsCompactOperatordefMathlib.Analysis.Normed.Operator.Compact.Basic, predicate
Wording
f is a compact operator (from the name)
English
Let $f$ be a function from elements of $x$ to elements of $x_{1}$. Then $f$ is a compact operator, if there exists an element $K$ of $\wp x_{1}$, such that $K$ is compact and the preimage of $f$ and $K$ is an element of the neighbourhood filter of $0$.
Formula
$f \in x \rightarrow x_{1} \vdash \mathrm{IsCompactOperator} (f):\Leftrightarrow \exists K \in \wp x_{1} . \mathrm{IsCompact} (K)\wedge (\MembershipmemMACRoAA {\mathrm{nhds} (0)}{\operatorname{preimage} (f , K)})$
French
Soit $f$ une fonction des éléments de $x$ à des éléments de $x_{1}$. Alors $f$ est un opérateur compact s'il existe un élément $K$ de $\wp x_{1}$ tel que $K$ soit compact et la préimage de $f$ et $K$ soit un élément du filtre de voisinages de $0$.
compactOperatordefMathlib.Analysis.Normed.Operator.Compact.Basic, function or constant
Wording
the compact operator of x4 (from the name)
English
Let $x_{4}$ be a ring homomorphism of $x$ and $x_{1}$. Let $x_{5}$ and $x_{6}$ be sets. Then the compact operator of $x_{4}$ is a submodule of $x_{1}$ and the set of continuous linear maps of $x_{5}$ and $x_{6}$.
Formula
$x_{4} \in \mathrm{RingHom} (x , x_{1}), x_{5} , x_{6} \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \mathrm{compactOperator} (x_{4} , x_{5} , x_{6})\in \mathrm{Submodule} (x_{1} , \mathrm{ContinuousLinearMap} (x_{4} , x_{5} , x_{6}))$
French
Soit $x_{4}$ un homomorphisme d'anneaux de $x$ et $x_{1}$. Soient $x_{5}$ et $x_{6}$ des ensembles. Alors l'opérateur compact de $x_{4}$ est un sous-module de $x_{1}$ et l'ensemble d'applications linéaires continues de $x_{5}$ et $x_{6}$.

Mathlib.Analysis.Normed.Operator.Completeness

ContinuousLinearMap.ofMemClosureImageCoeBoundeddefMathlib.Analysis.Normed.Operator.Completeness, function or constant
Wording
the continuous linear map of f (from the name)
English
Let $f$ be a function from elements of $\mathrm{E'}$ to elements of $F$. Assume that $s$ is bounded. Assume that $f$ is an element of the closure of the image of $\operatorname{coe}$ and $s$. Then the continuous linear map of $f$ is a continuous linear map of $\mathrm{E'}$ and $F$.
Formula
$f \in \mathrm{E'} \rightarrow F , \operatorname{IsBounded} (s), \MembershipmemMACRoAA {\mathrm{closure} (\operatorname{image} (\operatorname{coe} , s))}{f}\vdash \operatorname{ofMemClosureImageCoeBounded} (f , \mathrm{hs} , \mathrm{hf})\in \mathrm{ContinuousLinearMap} (x_{6} , \mathrm{E'} , F)$
French
Soit $f$ une fonction des éléments de $\mathrm{E'}$ à des éléments de $F$. Supposons que $s$ est borné. Supposons que $f$ est un élément de la clôture de l'image de $\operatorname{coe}$ et $s$. Alors l'application linéaire continue de $f$ est une application linéaire continue de $\mathrm{E'}$ et $F$.
ContinuousLinearMap.ofTendstoOfBoundedRangedefMathlib.Analysis.Normed.Operator.Completeness, function or constant
Wording
the continuous linear map of f and g (from the name)
English
Let $f$ be a function from elements of $\mathrm{E'}$ to elements of $F$. Let $g$ be a function from elements of $x_{9}$ to continuous linear maps of $\mathrm{E'}$ and $F$ such that $\operatorname{Tendsto}$ holds for the function that maps $a$ and $x_{10}$ to $g$ applied to $a$ and $x_{10}$, $l$ and the neighbourhood filter of $f$ and the range of $g$ is bounded. Then the continuous linear map of $f$ and $g$ is a continuous linear map of $\mathrm{E'}$ and $F$.
Formula
$f \in \mathrm{E'} \rightarrow F , g \in x_{9} \rightarrow \mathrm{ContinuousLinearMap} (x_{6} , \mathrm{E'} , F), \operatorname{Tendsto} (\lambda a , x_{10} . g (a , x_{10}), l , \mathrm{nhds} (f)), \operatorname{IsBounded} (\operatorname{range} (g))\vdash \operatorname{ofTendstoOfBoundedRange} (f , g , \mathrm{hf} , \mathrm{hg})\in \mathrm{ContinuousLinearMap} (x_{6} , \mathrm{E'} , F)$
French
Soit $f$ une fonction des éléments de $\mathrm{E'}$ à des éléments de $F$. Soit $g$ une fonction des éléments de $x_{9}$ à des applications linéaires continues de $\mathrm{E'}$ et $F$ telle que $\operatorname{Tendsto}$ tienne pour la fonction qui envoie $a$ et $x_{10}$ à $g$ appliqué à $a$ et $x_{10}$, $l$ et le filtre de voisinages de $f$ et l'image de $g$ soit bornée. Alors l'application linéaire continue de $f$ et $g$ est une application linéaire continue de $\mathrm{E'}$ et $F$.

Mathlib.Analysis.Normed.Operator.Conformal

IsConformalMapdefMathlib.Analysis.Normed.Operator.Conformal, predicate
Wording
#1 is a conformal map (from the name)
English
Let $\mathrm{f'}$ be a continuous linear map of $X$ and $Y$. Then $\mathrm{f'}$ is a conformal map, if there exists an element $c$ of $R$, such that $c \neq 0$ and there exists a linear isometry $\mathrm{li}$ of $X$ and $Y$ such that $\EqtMACRoH {\mathrm{f'}}{\HSMulhSMulMACRoAL {c}{\operatorname{toContinuousLinearMap} (\mathrm{li})}}$.
Formula
$\mathrm{f'} \in \mathrm{ContinuousLinearMap} (\operatorname{id} (R), X , Y)\vdash \mathrm{IsConformalMap} (\mathrm{f'}):\Leftrightarrow \exists c \in R . c \neq 0 \wedge (\exists \mathrm{li} \in \mathrm{LinearIsometry} (\operatorname{id} (R), X , Y). \EqtMACRoJ {\mathrm{f'}}{\HSMulhSMulMACRoAT {c}{\operatorname{toContinuousLinearMap} (\mathrm{li})}})$
French
Soit $\mathrm{f'}$ une application linéaire continue de $X$ et $Y$. Alors $\mathrm{f'}$ est une application conforme s'il existe un élément $c$ de $R$ tel que $c \neq 0$ et il existe une isométrie linéaire $\mathrm{li}$ de $X$ et $Y$ telle que $\EqtMACRoH {\mathrm{f'}}{\HSMulhSMulMACRoAL {c}{\operatorname{toContinuousLinearMap} (\mathrm{li})}}$.

Mathlib.Analysis.Normed.Operator.ContinuousLinearMap

ContinuousLinearEquiv.ofHomothetydefMathlib.Analysis.Normed.Operator.ContinuousLinearMap, function or constant
Wording
the continuous linear equivalence of f and a (from the name)
English
Let $f$ be a linear equivalence of $E$ and $F$. Let $a$ be a real number such that $0 < a$ and for all elements $x_{10}$ of $E$, the norm of $f$ applied to $x_{10}$ is equal to the product of $a$ and the norm of $x_{10}$. Then the continuous linear equivalence of $f$ and $a$ is a continuous linear equivalence of $E$ and $F$.
Formula
$f \in \mathrm{LinearEquiv} (x_{8} , E , F), a \in \Real , 0 < a , \forall x_{10} \in E . \operatorname{norm} (f (x_{10}))= a \times \operatorname{norm} (x_{10})\vdash \operatorname{ofHomothety} (f , a , \mathrm{ha} , \mathrm{hf})\in \mathrm{ContinuousLinearEquiv} (x_{8} , E , F)$
French
Soit $f$ une équivalence linéaire de $E$ et $F$. Soit $a$ un nombre réel tel que $0 < a$ et pour tous des éléments $x_{10}$ de $E$, la norme de $f$ appliqué à $x_{10}$ soit égale au produit de $a$ et de la norme de $x_{10}$. Alors l'équivalence linéaire continue de $f$ et $a$ est une équivalence linéaire continue de $E$ et $F$.

LinearEquiv.toContinuousLinearEquivOfBoundsdefMathlib.Analysis.Normed.Operator.ContinuousLinearMap, function or constant
Wording
the continuous linear equivalence of C_to and C_inv (from the name)
English
Let $e$ be a linear equivalence of $E$ and $F$. Let $\mathrm{C\_to}$ and $\mathrm{C\_inv}$ be real numbers such that for all elements $x_{11}$ of $E$, the norm of $e$ applied to $x_{11}$ is less than or equal to the product of $\mathrm{C\_to}$ and the norm of $x_{11}$ and for all elements $x_{10}$ of $F$, the norm of $\operatorname{symm}$ applied to $e$ and $x_{10}$ is less than or equal to the product of $\mathrm{C\_inv}$ and the norm of $x_{10}$. Then the continuous linear equivalence of $\mathrm{C\_to}$ and $\mathrm{C\_inv}$ is a continuous linear equivalence of $E$ and $F$.
Formula
$e \in \mathrm{LinearEquiv} (x_{8} , E , F), \mathrm{C\_to} , \mathrm{C\_inv} \in \Real , \forall x_{11} \in E . \operatorname{norm} (e (x_{11}))\leq \mathrm{C\_to} \times \operatorname{norm} (x_{11}), \forall x_{10} \in F . \operatorname{norm} (\operatorname{symm} (e , x_{10}))\leq \mathrm{C\_inv} \times \operatorname{norm} (x_{10})\vdash \operatorname{toContinuousLinearEquivOfBounds} (e , \mathrm{C\_to} , \mathrm{C\_inv} , \mathrm{h\_to} , \mathrm{h\_inv})\in \mathrm{ContinuousLinearEquiv} (x_{8} , E , F)$
French
Soit $e$ une équivalence linéaire de $E$ et $F$. Soient $\mathrm{C\_to}$ et $\mathrm{C\_inv}$ des nombres réels tels que pour tous des éléments $x_{11}$ de $E$, la norme de $e$ appliqué à $x_{11}$ soit inférieure ou égale au produit de $\mathrm{C\_to}$ et de la norme de $x_{11}$ et pour tous des éléments $x_{10}$ de $F$, la norme de $\operatorname{symm}$ appliqué à $e$ et $x_{10}$ soit inférieure ou égale au produit de $\mathrm{C\_inv}$ et de la norme de $x_{10}$. Alors l'équivalence linéaire continue de $\mathrm{C\_to}$ et $\mathrm{C\_inv}$ est une équivalence linéaire continue de $E$ et $F$.

no wording

Mathlib.Analysis.Normed.Operator.Extend

LinearEquiv.extenddefMathlib.Analysis.Normed.Operator.Extend, function or constant
Wording
the extension of x16 and x17 (from the name, with words of the lexicon)
English
Let $f$ be a linear equivalence of $E$ and $F$. Let $x_{16}$ be a linear map of $E$ and $x_{2}$. Let $x_{17}$ be a linear map of $F$ and $x_{3}$. Assume that $\operatorname{coe}$ applied to $x_{16}$ is a dense range. Assume that there exists a real number $C_{1}$, such that for all elements $x_{19}$ of $E$, the norm of $x_{17}$ applied to $f$ applied to $x_{19}$ is less than or equal to the product of $C_{1}$ and the norm of $x_{16}$ applied to $x_{19}$. Assume that $\operatorname{coe}$ applied to $x_{17}$ is a dense range. Assume that there exists a real number $C$, such that for all elements $x_{18}$ of $F$, the norm of $x_{16}$ applied to $\operatorname{symm}$ applied to $f$ and $x_{18}$ is less than or equal to the product of $C$ and the norm of $x_{17}$ applied to $x_{18}$. Then the extension of $x_{16}$ and $x_{17}$ is a continuous linear equivalence of $x_{2}$ and $x_{3}$.
Formula
$f \in \mathrm{LinearEquiv} (x_{14} , E , F), x_{16} \in \mathrm{LinearMap} (\operatorname{id} (x), E , x_{2}), x_{17} \in \mathrm{LinearMap} (\operatorname{id} (x_{1}), F , x_{3}), \mathrm{DenseRange} (\operatorname{coe} (x_{16})), \exists C_{1} \in \Real . \forall x_{19} \in E . \operatorname{norm} (x_{17} (f (x_{19})))\leq C_{1} \times \operatorname{norm} (x_{16} (x_{19})), \mathrm{DenseRange} (\operatorname{coe} (x_{17})), \exists C \in \Real . \forall x_{18} \in F . \operatorname{norm} (x_{16} (\operatorname{symm} (f , x_{18})))\leq C \times \operatorname{norm} (x_{17} (x_{18}))\vdash \operatorname{extend} (f , x_{16} , x_{17} , h_{6} , h_{7} , h_{8} , h_{9})\in \mathrm{ContinuousLinearEquiv} (x_{14} , x_{2} , x_{3})$
French
Soit $f$ une équivalence linéaire de $E$ et $F$. Soit $x_{16}$ une application linéaire de $E$ et $x_{2}$. Soit $x_{17}$ une application linéaire de $F$ et $x_{3}$. Supposons que $\operatorname{coe}$ appliqué à $x_{16}$ est une image dense. Supposons qu'il existe un nombre réel $C_{1}$ tel que pour tous les éléments $x_{19}$ de $E$, la norme de $x_{17}$ appliqué à $f$ appliqué à $x_{19}$ soit inférieure ou égale au produit de $C_{1}$ et de la norme de $x_{16}$ appliqué à $x_{19}$. Supposons que $\operatorname{coe}$ appliqué à $x_{17}$ est une image dense. Supposons qu'il existe un nombre réel $C$ tel que pour tous les éléments $x_{18}$ de $F$, la norme de $x_{16}$ appliqué à $\operatorname{symm}$ appliqué à $f$ et $x_{18}$ soit inférieure ou égale au produit de $C$ et de la norme de $x_{17}$ appliqué à $x_{18}$. Alors l'extension de $x_{16}$ et $x_{17}$ est une équivalence linéaire continue de $x_{2}$ et $x_{3}$.
LinearEquiv.extendOfIsometrydefMathlib.Analysis.Normed.Operator.Extend, function or constant
Wording
the extension of x16 and x17 (from the name, with words of the lexicon)
English
Let $f$ be a linear equivalence of $E$ and $F$. Let $x_{16}$ be a linear map of $E$ and $x_{2}$. Let $x_{17}$ be a linear map of $F$ and $x_{3}$. Assume that $\operatorname{coe}$ applied to $x_{16}$ is a dense range. Assume that $\operatorname{coe}$ applied to $x_{17}$ is a dense range. Assume that for all elements $x_{18}$ of $E$, the norm of $x_{17}$ applied to $f$ applied to $x_{18}$ is equal to the norm of $x_{16}$ applied to $x_{18}$. Then the extension of $x_{16}$ and $x_{17}$ is a linear isometry equivalence of $x_{2}$ and $x_{3}$.
Formula
$f \in \mathrm{LinearEquiv} (x_{14} , E , F), x_{16} \in \mathrm{LinearMap} (\operatorname{id} (x), E , x_{2}), x_{17} \in \mathrm{LinearMap} (\operatorname{id} (x_{1}), F , x_{3}), \mathrm{DenseRange} (\operatorname{coe} (x_{16})), \mathrm{DenseRange} (\operatorname{coe} (x_{17})), \forall x_{18} \in E . \operatorname{norm} (x_{17} (f (x_{18})))= \operatorname{norm} (x_{16} (x_{18}))\vdash \operatorname{extendOfIsometry} (f , x_{16} , x_{17} , h_{4} , h_{5} , \mathrm{h\_norm})\in \mathrm{LinearIsometryEquiv} (x_{14} , x_{2} , x_{3})$
French

Supposons que $\operatorname{coe}$ appliqué à $x_{16}$ est une image dense. Supposons que $\operatorname{coe}$ appliqué à $x_{17}$ est une image dense. Supposons que pour tous les éléments $x_{18}$ de $E$, la norme de $x_{17}$ appliqué à $f$ appliqué à $x_{18}$ est égale à la norme de $x_{16}$ appliqué à $x_{18}$. Alors l'extension de $x_{16}$ et $x_{17}$ est une équivalence d'isométries linéaire de $x_{2}$ et $x_{3}$.

Wording
the extension of f (from the name, with words of the lexicon)

Soit $v$ une valeur absolue de $R$ et $S$. Soit $w$ une valeur absolue de $T$ et $S$. Soit $f$ un homomorphisme d'anneaux de $R$ et $T$. Alors l'application de $w$ et $f$ est un homomorphisme d'anneaux de $\mathrm{WithAbs}$ appliqué à $v$ et $\mathrm{WithAbs}$ appliqué à $w$ défini comme la composition de l'homomorphisme d'anneaux de l'inverse de l'équivalence de $w$ et la composition de $f$ et l'homomorphisme d'anneaux de l'équivalence de $v$.

normRingSeminormdefMathlib.Analysis.Normed.Unbundled.RingSeminorm, function or constant
Wording
the norm of R (from the docstring)
English
Let $R$ be a set. Then the norm of $R$ is a ring seminorm of $R$.
Formula
$R \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \mathrm{normRingSeminorm} (R)\in \mathrm{RingSeminorm} (R)$
French
Soit $R$ un ensemble. Alors la norme de $R$ est une semi-norme d'anneaux de $R$.

normFromBoundeddefMathlib.Analysis.Normed.Unbundled.SeminormFromBounded, function or constant
Wording
no wording
English
Assume that $f (0)= 0$. Assume that $0 \leq f$. Assume that for all elements $x_{2}$ and $y$ of $R$, $f (x_{2} \times y)\leq c \times f (x_{2})\times f (y)$. Assume that for all elements $a$ and $b$ of $R$, $f (a + b)\leq f (a)+ f (b)$. Assume that for all elements $x_{1}$ of $R$, $f (\operatorname{neg} (x_{1}))= f (x_{1})$. Assume that the preimage of $f$ and $\operatorname{singleton}$ applied to $0$ is equal to $\operatorname{singleton}$ applied to $0$. Then $\mathrm{normFromBounded}$ applied to $\mathrm{f\_zero}$, $\mathrm{f\_nonneg}$, $\mathrm{f\_mul}$, $\mathrm{f\_add}$, $\mathrm{f\_neg}$ and $\mathrm{f\_ker}$ is a ring norm of $R$.
Formula

seminormFromBounded'defMathlib.Analysis.Normed.Unbundled.SeminormFromBounded, function or constant
Wording
no wording
English
Let $f$ be a function from elements of $R$ to real numbers. Let $x_{1}$ be an element of $R$. Then $\mathrm{seminormFromBounded'}$ applied to $f$ and $x_{1}$ is a real number defined as the supremum of the function that maps $y$ to $\frac{ f (x_{1} \times y)}{f (y)}$.
Formula
$f \in R \rightarrow \Real , x_{1} \in R \vdash \mathrm{seminormFromBounded'} (f , x_{1}):= \mathrm{iSup} (\lambda y . \frac{ f (x_{1} \times y)}{f (y)})\in \Real$
French
Soit $f$ une fonction des éléments de $R$ à des nombres réels. Soit $x_{1}$ un élément de $R$. Alors $\mathrm{seminormFromBounded'}$ appliqué à $f$ et $x_{1}$ est un nombre réel défini comme le supremum de la fonction qui envoie $y$ à $\frac{ f (x_{1} \times y)}{f (y)}$.

Wording

English

Let $c$ and $x_{1}$ be elements of $R$. Let $f$ be a ring seminorm of $R$. Then the seminorm from constant of $f$ and $x_{1}$ is a real number defined as the infimum of $\mathrm{seminormFromConst\_seq}$ applied to $x_{1}$.

seminormFromConst_seqdefMathlib.Analysis.Normed.Unbundled.SeminormFromConst, function or constant
Wording
the seminorm from constant sequence of x1 and x2 (from the name)
English
Let $c$ and $x_{1}$ be elements of $R$. Let $f$ be a ring seminorm of $R$. Let $x_{2}$ be a natural number. Then the seminorm from constant sequence of $x_{1}$ and $x_{2}$ is a real number defined as $\frac{ f (x_{1} \times c ^ {x_{2}})}{f (c)^ {x_{2}}}$.
Formula
$c \in R , f \in \mathrm{RingSeminorm} (R), x_{1} \in R , x_{2} \in \Natural \vdash \mathrm{seminormFromConst\_seq} (c , f , x_{1} , x_{2}):= \frac{ f (x_{1} \times c ^ {x_{2}})}{f (c)^ {x_{2}}}\in \Real$
French

Mathlib.Analysis.Normed.Unbundled.SmoothingSeminorm

smoothingFundefMathlib.Analysis.Normed.Unbundled.SmoothingSeminorm, function or constant
Wording
no wording
English
Let $x_{1}$ be a ring seminorm of $R$. Let $x_{2}$ be an element of $R$. Then $\mathrm{smoothingFun}$ applied to $x_{1}$ and $x_{2}$ is a real number defined as the infimum of the function that maps $n$ to the exponentiation of $x_{1}$ applied to the exponentiation of $x_{2}$ to the value of $n$ to the quotient of $1$ and the value of $n$.
Formula
$x_{1} \in \mathrm{RingSeminorm} (R), x_{2} \in R \vdash \mathrm{smoothingFun} (x_{1} , x_{2}):= \mathrm{iInf} (\lambda n . x_{1} (x_{2} ^ {\operatorname{val} (n)})^ {\frac{ 1}{\operatorname{val} (n)}})\in \Real$
French
Soit $x_{1}$ une semi-norme d'anneaux de $R$. Soit $x_{2}$ un élément de $R$. Alors $\mathrm{smoothingFun}$ appliqué à $x_{1}$ et $x_{2}$ est un nombre réel défini comme l'infimum de la fonction qui envoie $n$ à la puissance de $x_{1}$ appliqué à la puissance de $x_{2}$ en la valeur de $n$ en le quotient entre $1$ et la valeur de $n$.
smoothingSeminormdefMathlib.Analysis.Normed.Unbundled.SmoothingSeminorm, function or constant
Wording
no wording
English
Let $x_{1}$ be a ring seminorm of $R$ such that $x_{1} (1)\leq 1$ and $\operatorname{coe}$ applied to $x_{1}$ is a nonarchimedean. Then $\mathrm{smoothingSeminorm}$ applied to $x_{1}$, $h$ and $\mathrm{hna}$ is a ring seminorm of $R$.
Formula
$x_{1} \in \mathrm{RingSeminorm} (R), x_{1} (1)\leq 1 , \mathrm{IsNonarchimedean} (\operatorname{coe} (x_{1}))\vdash \mathrm{smoothingSeminorm} (x_{1} , h , \mathrm{hna})\in \mathrm{RingSeminorm} (R)$
French
Soit $x_{1}$ une semi-norme d'anneaux de $R$ telle que $x_{1} (1)\leq 1$ et $\operatorname{coe}$ appliqué à $x_{1}$ soit un non archimédien. Alors $\mathrm{smoothingSeminorm}$ appliqué à $x_{1}$, $h$ et $\mathrm{hna}$ est une semi-norme d'anneaux de $R$.
smoothingSeminormSeqdefMathlib.Analysis.Normed.Unbundled.SmoothingSeminorm, function or constant
Wording
no wording
English
Let $x_{1}$ be a ring seminorm of $R$. Let $x_{2}$ be an element of $R$. Let $x_{3}$ be a natural number. Then $\mathrm{smoothingSeminormSeq}$ applied to $x_{1}$, $x_{2}$ and $x_{3}$ is a real number defined as $x_{1} (x_{2} ^ {x_{3}})^ {\frac{ 1}{x_{3}}}$.
